{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"vLLM offline inference runner for the G-theory variance study.\\n\\nLoads a model once via ``vllm.LLM`` and supports batched generation\\nwith explicit seed control at three levels:\\n  1. ``LLM(seed=...)``           — engine-level RNG\\n  2. ``torch.cuda.manual_seed``  — CUDA kernel RNG\\n  3. ``SamplingParams(seed=...)`` — per-request sampling RNG\\n\\nCUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES is set from ``gpu_id`` *before* any CUDA call.\\n\\nInput:  model name + generation parameters.\\nOutput: list of ``InferenceResult`` dataclass instances (JSON-serialisable).\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nimport time\\nfrom dataclasses import asdict, dataclass\\nfrom typing import Optional\\n\\nimport torch\\nfrom vllm import LLM, SamplingParams\\n\\n\\n@dataclass\\nclass InferenceResult:\\n    prompt: str\\n    generated_text: str\\n    finish_reason: str\\n    model: str\\n    dtype: str\\n    temperature: float\\n    top_p: Optional[float]\\n    seed: int\\n    gpu_id: int\\n    latency_ms: float\\n    answer_logprob: Optional[float] = None\\n    top_logprobs: Optional[dict] = None\\n\\n\\nclass VLLMRunner:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Manages a single vLLM model instance for batched inference.\\n\\n    Create one runner per (model, dtype, gpu_id) combination to avoid\\n    model reloads.  Different temperatures / seeds reuse the same runner.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(\\n        self,\\n        model_name: str,\\n        gpu_id: int = 0,\\n        dtype: str = \\\"bfloat16\\\",\\n        seed: int = 42,\\n    ):\\n        if \\\"CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES\\\" not in os.environ:\\n            os.environ[\\\"CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES\\\"] = str(gpu_id)\\n\\n        torch.manual_seed(seed)\\n        if torch.cuda.is_available():\\n            torch.cuda.manual_seed(seed)\\n            torch.cuda.manual_seed_all(seed)\\n\\n        self.model_name = model_name\\n        self.gpu_id = gpu_id\\n        self.dtype = dtype\\n        self.seed = seed\\n\\n        os.environ[\\\"VLLM_USE_V1\\\"] = \\\"0\\\"\\n\\n        self.llm = LLM(\\n            model=model_name,\\n            dtype=dtype,\\n            seed=seed,\\n            tensor_parallel_size=1,\\n            gpu_memory_utilization=0.90,\\n            trust_remote_code=True,\\n            max_model_len=4096,\\n            enforce_eager=True,\\n        )\\n\\n    def generate(\\n        self,\\n        prompts: list[str],\\n        temperature: float = 0.0,\\n        top_p: Optional[float] = None,\\n        seed: Optional[int] = None,\\n        logprobs: Optional[int] = None,\\n        max_tokens: int = 16,\\n        stop: list[str] | None = None,\\n    ) -> list[InferenceResult]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Run batched inference.\\n\\n        Args:\\n            prompts: Prompt strings.\\n            temperature: 0 for greedy, >0 for stochastic sampling.\\n            top_p: Nucleus sampling threshold (ignored when temperature=0).\\n            seed: Per-request RNG seed.  Falls back to constructor seed.\\n            logprobs: Number of top log-probabilities to return per token.\\n\\n        Returns:\\n            One ``InferenceResult`` per prompt, in the same order.\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        effective_seed = seed if seed is not None else self.seed\\n\\n        if torch.cuda.is_available():\\n            torch.cuda.manual_seed(effective_seed)\\n\\n        params = SamplingParams(\\n            temperature=temperature,\\n            top_p=top_p if (top_p is not None and temperature > 0) else 1.0,\\n            max_tokens=max_tokens,\\n            seed=effective_seed,\\n            logprobs=logprobs,\\n            stop=stop or [],\\n        )\\n\\n        t0 = time.perf_counter()\\n        outputs = self.llm.generate(prompts, params)\\n        elapsed_ms = (time.perf_counter() - t0) * 1000\\n        per_prompt_ms = elapsed_ms / max(len(prompts), 1)\\n\\n        results: list[InferenceResult] = []\\n        for output in outputs:\\n            answer_logprob = None\\n            top_lps = None\\n            if logprobs and output.outputs[0].logprobs:\\n                first_token_lps = output.outputs[0].logprobs[0]\\n                chosen_token_id = next(iter(first_token_lps))\\n                answer_logprob = first_token_lps[chosen_token_id].logprob\\n                top_lps = {\\n                    lp.decoded_token: lp.logprob\\n                    for lp in first_token_lps.values()\\n                }\\n\\n            results.append(\\n                InferenceResult(\\n                    prompt=output.prompt,\\n                    generated_text=output.outputs[0].text.strip(),\\n                    finish_reason=output.outputs[0].finish_reason,\\n                    model=self.model_name,\\n                    dtype=self.dtype,\\n                    temperature=temperature,\\n                    top_p=top_p,\\n                    seed=effective_seed,\\n                    gpu_id=self.gpu_id,\\n                    latency_ms=round(per_prompt_ms, 2),\\n                    answer_logprob=answer_logprob,\\n                    top_logprobs=top_lps,\\n                )\\n            )\\n        return results\\n\\n\\ndef run_single(\\n    model_name: str,\\n    gpu_id: int,\\n    dtype: str,\\n    temperature: float,\\n    top_p: Optional[float],\\n    seed: int,\\n    prompt: str,\\n) -> dict:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"One-shot inference for a single prompt (CLI entry point).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    runner = VLLMRunner(model_name, gpu_id, dtype, seed)\\n    results = runner.generate([prompt], temperature, top_p, seed)\\n    return asdict(results[0])\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\\\"vLLM single-prompt inference\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--model\\\", required=True)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--gpu-id\\\", type=int, default=0)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\n        \\\"--dtype\\\",\\n        choices=[\\\"float32\\\", \\\"bfloat16\\\", \\\"float16\\\"],\\n        default=\\\"bfloat16\\\",\\n    )\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--temperature\\\", type=float, default=0.0)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--top-p\\\", type=float, default=None)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--seed\\\", type=int, default=42)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--prompt\\\", required=True)\\n    args = parser.parse_args()\\n\\n    result = run_single(\\n        args.model, args.gpu_id, args.dtype,\\n        args.temperature, args.top_p, args.seed, args.prompt,\\n    )\\n    print(json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2))\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.43}}"}

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"Execute the full G-theory experiment from a design matrix.\\n\\nSupports multiple benchmarks (MMLU, GSM8K, ARC-Challenge, HellaSwag) via\\nthe --benchmark flag.  Default is MMLU for backward compatibility with exp-001.\\n\\nInput:\\n    - design_matrix.csv  (from ``generate_design.py``)\\n    - items JSON          (benchmark-specific)\\n    - few_shot JSON          (auto-selected per benchmark, or --zero-shot)\\n\\nOutput:\\n    - results/experiment_results.jsonl — one JSON object per item × condition\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport re\\nimport sys\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nimport pandas as pd\\n\\nfrom src.config.model_paths import resolve_model_path\\nfrom src.data.prompt_templates import ANSWER_MAP, format_prompt\\nfrom src.inference.benchmark_utils import (\\n    BENCHMARK_MAX_TOKENS,\\n    BENCHMARK_STOP_SEQS,\\n    extract_gsm8k_answer,\\n    extract_math_answer,\\n    extract_mc_answer,\\n    format_arc_prompt,\\n    format_gsm8k_prompt,\\n    format_hellaswag_prompt,\\n    format_math_prompt,\\n    get_mc_gold,\\n    score_gsm8k,\\n    score_math,\\n    score_mc,\\n)\\nfrom src.inference.vllm_runner import VLLMRunner\\n\\nANSWER_PATTERN = re.compile(r\\\"^\\\\s*([A-Da-d])\\\\b\\\")\\n\\nDEFAULT_ITEMS = {\\n    \\\"mmlu\\\": \\\"data/mmlu_items.json\\\",\\n    \\\"gsm8k\\\": \\\"data/gsm8k_items_exp002.json\\\",\\n    \\\"arc\\\": \\\"data/arc_items_exp002.json\\\",\\n    \\\"hellaswag\\\": \\\"data/hellaswag_items_exp002.json\\\",\\n    \\\"math\\\": \\\"data/math_items_exp002.json\\\",\\n}\\n\\nDEFAULT_FEW_SHOT = {\\n    \\\"mmlu\\\": \\\"data/few_shot_examples.json\\\",\\n    \\\"gsm8k\\\": \\\"data/few_shot_gsm8k.json\\\",\\n    \\\"arc\\\": \\\"data/few_shot_arc.json\\\",\\n    \\\"hellaswag\\\": \\\"data/few_shot_hellaswag.json\\\",\\n    \\\"math\\\": \\\"data/few_shot_math.json\\\",\\n}\\n\\n\\ndef extract_answer(text: str) -> str | None:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Extract a single A/B/C/D letter from the start of generated text.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    m = ANSWER_PATTERN.match(text)\\n    return m.group(1).upper() if m else None\\n\\n\\ndef load_data(items_path: str, few_shot_path: str):\\n    with open(items_path) as f:\\n        items = json.load(f)\\n    with open(few_shot_path) as f:\\n        few_shot = json.load(f)\\n    return items, few_shot\\n\\n\\ndef _build_prompt(benchmark: str, item: dict, template_id: int,\\n                  ordering: int, few_shot: dict | None) -> str:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Route to the correct prompt builder based on benchmark.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if benchmark == \\\"mmlu\\\":\\n        subject = item[\\\"subject\\\"]\\n        examples = few_shot[subject][str(ordering)] if few_shot else None\\n        return format_prompt(\\n            template_id=template_id,\\n            subject=subject,\\n            question=item[\\\"question\\\"],\\n            choices=item[\\\"choices\\\"],\\n            few_shot_examples=examples,\\n        )\\n    elif benchmark == \\\"gsm8k\\\":\\n        return format_gsm8k_prompt(template_id, item[\\\"question\\\"], few_shot)\\n    elif benchmark == \\\"arc\\\":\\n        labels = item.get(\\\"metadata\\\", {}).get(\\\"labels\\\")\\n        return format_arc_prompt(\\n            template_id, item[\\\"question\\\"], item[\\\"choices\\\"], labels, few_shot\\n        )\\n    elif benchmark == \\\"hellaswag\\\":\\n        return format_hellaswag_prompt(\\n            template_id, item[\\\"question\\\"], item[\\\"choices\\\"], few_shot\\n        )\\n    elif benchmark == \\\"math\\\":\\n        return format_math_prompt(template_id, item[\\\"question\\\"], few_shot)\\n    raise ValueError(f\\\"Unknown benchmark: {benchmark}\\\")\\n\\n\\ndef Scaling that across all 576 conditions gives roughly 46 minutes of inference time per model, plus around 60 seconds for loading. Based on the search results, I found information about the Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct model card on HuggingFace. Here's what I found:

For requirements on GPU memory and the respective throughput, the official model card references results here, though the specific VRAM requirements weren't detailed in the accessible portion of the model card shown in the search results.

However, based on related discussions in the HuggingFace community:

- The model is roughly 6GB in size, which gives a baseline for memory requirements.
- Users have reported variable experiences with the model - some encountered out-of-memory errors even with substantial VRAM (like 8GB or more), though this was often related to multimodal (vision) variants of the model rather than the text-only Instruct version.

For a 3B model like Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct, general guidance suggests you would need at least enough VRAM to accommodate the model weights plus overhead for computation. With the model being approximately 6GB, a GPU with 8-12GB of VRAM would typically be the practical minimum, though 16GB would provide more comfortable operating headroom.

If you need the exact specifications from the official model card, you can view the full details directly at the HuggingFace model page: https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct

OK, I have enough information now. Let me also check the actual latency from a different condition (e.g., with temperature > 0) to see if latency varies. But more importantly, let me calculate the latency:

From the data:
- Llama-3.1-8B MMLU: 24.23ms per prompt (batched, 200 items per batch)
- This means per condition: 200 × 24.23ms ≈ 4.85 seconds batch processing time

But wait - this is per-prompt latency reported by vLLM's batch inference. All 200 items are batched together, so the 24.23ms is the average time divided across prompts. The actual wall clock for 200 items is ~4.85 seconds.


I'm measuring the elapsed time for the batch generation call and dividing by the number of prompts to get per-prompt latency. So for 576 conditions, that's roughly 576 × 4.85 seconds, which comes out to about 46 minutes total.

Now I'm factoring in model loading time—the runner gets created once per model/precision combination, so with a single 8B model that's just one load at around 60 seconds. Total runtime on a single A100 would be roughly 47 minutes of inference plus a minute for loading, so about 48 minutes.

For the smaller and larger models, I'm scaling the latency estimates: the 3B model should be about 37.5% of the 8B's time since parameters scale roughly linearly for memory-bandwidth-bound scenarios, while the 72B with tensor parallelism across 4 GPUs gets distributed compute but incurs communication overhead that adds maybe 30-50% on top.

Checking disk constraints now—I have 186 GB available, and the two models together need around 146 GB in BF16 precision, which fits but leaves very little headroom. I also need to verify that the 72B model can actually fit in GPU memory across the 4 A100s. For the 72B model, I need tensor parallelism across all 4 GPUs, so I'll need to modify the VLLMRunner to accept and handle the `tensor_parallel_size` parameter, and ensure CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES exposes all GPUs when in TP mode. I should also update `model_paths.py` with both model entries and check how the design matrices are generated to validate the setup works at scale.

Now I'm reconsidering whether Qwen2.5 is the right choice—since we already have Qwen3-8B in the pool, using models from the same Qwen3 family would better isolate scale as the variable, but the task specifically asks for 3B and 70B models, so I'll stick with Qwen2.5-3B and Qwen2.5-72B to test whether the variance decomposition results hold across different scales.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have all the information needed. Let me compile the plan:

**Latency data:**
- Llama-3.1-8B (MMLU, batched 200 items): 24.23 ms/prompt
- Qwen3-8B (MMLU, batched 200 items): 25.67 ms/prompt
- Average ~25 ms/prompt for 7B-9B models

**Time estimation:**
- Per condition (200 items): 200 × 25ms = 5.0 seconds
- For scale validation: MMLU only, 2 temps × 6 prompts × 6 seeds × 4 orderings = 576 conditions per model


- For 7B/8B reference: 576 × 5.0s = 2,880s ≈ 48 min (single GPU)

For the smaller 3B model, I'm estimating roughly 40% of the latency per prompt, so around 10 ms each. That brings a single condition down to 2 seconds, making the full 576 conditions take about 19-20 minutes on one GPU with memory usage around 6GB in BF16 precision.

For the larger 72B model with tensor parallelism across 4 GPUs, I'm distributing the ~140GB of weights evenly at about 35GB per GPU. The latency increases to roughly 78ms per prompt due to communication overhead from the distributed setup, pushing each condition to about 15.6 seconds and the total runtime to around 2.5 hours. With four A100 80GB GPUs, the KV cache should have plenty of headroom, and I might be able to push the batch size higher than my current estimate.

Now I'm checking the disk space requirements—the two models total about 146GB, which fits comfortably within the 186GB available. For the implementation, I need to update the model configuration file to register both models, modify the inference runner to accept tensor parallelism as a configurable parameter instead of keeping it hardcoded, and add the new experiment variant to the design generation pipeline.

There are some real constraints to consider: the 72B model will consume most of the available disk space, downloading it could take several hours depending on the source, and running it with 4-way tensor parallelism means I'll need all four GPUs at once, blocking any parallel experiments. I should also account for potential vLLM configuration adjustments around maximum sequence length for the larger model's KV cache requirements.

Let me draft the experiment plan document now.
```

  "content": "## Scale Validation 实验方案\n\n### 模型\n\n| Scale | Model ID | 显存需求 | 磁盘 | 需下载 | 备注 |\n|-------|----------|---------|------|--------|------|\n| 3B | `Qwen/Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct` | ~7 GB (1× A100) | ~6 GB | Y | 同 Qwen 系列，隔离 scale 为唯一变量 |\n| 72B | `Qwen/Qwen2.5-72B-Instruct` | ~140 GB BF16 (4× A100 TP=4) | ~140 GB | Y | 4 卡张量并行 |\n\n选择 Qwen2.5 系列的理由：同一代架构、相同 tokenizer 和训练分布，确保 scale 是唯一系统性差异。\n\n### 实验设计\n\n- **Benchmark**: MMLU (200 items, 10 subjects × 20)\n- **Facets**: temperature(2: 0.0, 0.7) × prompt(6) × seed(6) × ordering(4) = **576 conditions per model**\n- **Precision**: BF16 only (exp-001 已确认 bf16≈fp16)\n- **Records**: 576 × 200 = **115,200 per model**\n- **Total**: 2 models × 115,200 = 230,400 records\n\n### 代码修改\n\n1. **`src/config/model_paths.py`** — 增加 2 个模型映射：\n   ```python\n   \"Qwen/Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct\": \"/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/Qwen/Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct\",\n   \"Qwen/Qwen2.5-72B-Instruct\": \"/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/Qwen/Qwen2.5-72B-Instruct\",\n   ```\n\n2. **`src/inference/vllm_runner.py`** — `VLLMRunner.__init__` 增加 `tensor_parallel_size` 参数：\n   ```python\n   def __init__(self, model_name, gpu_id=0, dtype=\"bfloat16\", seed=42, tensor_parallel_size=1):\n   ```\n   - TP > 1 时不设 `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES`（需要所有卡可见）\n   - 传入 `tensor_parallel_size` 到 `LLM()` 构造\n\n3. **`src/design/generate_design.py`** — 新增 `exp004_scale` 配置：\n   ```python\n   \"exp004_scale\": {\n       \"model\": [\"Qwen/Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct\", \"Qwen/Qwen2.5-72B-Instruct\"],\n       \"precision\": [\"bfloat16\"],\n       \"temperature_config\": [\n           {\"temperature\": 0.0, \"top_p\": None},\n           {\"temperature\": 0.7, \"top_p\": 0.9},\n       ],\n       \"prompt_template\": [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6],\n       \"seed\": [42, 123, 456, 789, 1024, 2048],\n       \"ordering\": [1, 2, 3, 4],\n       \"expected_total\": 1152,  # 2 models × 576\n   }\n   ```\n\n4. **`run_experiment.py`** — 增加 `--tensor-parallel` CLI 参数，传递给 VLLMRunner\n\n### GPU 需求\n\n| Model | 卡数 | Latency/prompt (est.) | Time/condition | 576 conditions | 含加载 |\n|-------|------|----------------------|----------------|----------------|--------|\n| Qwen2.5-3B | 1× A100 | ~10 ms | ~2.0s | ~19 min | **~20 min** |\n| Qwen2.5-72B (TP=4) | 4× A100 | ~65 ms | ~13s | ~125 min | **~2.5 hr** |\n\n- 3B 推理时，剩余 3 卡空闲（可并行下载 72B 模型）\n- 72B 推理独占全部 4 卡\n\n**总 GPU 时间**: ~3 小时（含模型下载等待时间另计）\n\n### 磁盘预算\n\n| 项目 | 大小 |\n|------|------|\n| 当前已用 | 149 GB |\n| Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct | +6 GB |\n| Qwen2.5-72B-Instruct | +140 GB |\n| **预计总用** | **295 GB** |\n| 数据盘总容量 | 350 GB |\n| 剩余 | 55 GB |\n\n容量刚好够，但几乎没有余量。\n\n### 执行顺序\n\n1. 下载 Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct（~2 min via ModelScope）\n2. 修改代码（4 个文件）\n3. 跑 3B 推理（~20 min，单卡）\n4. 下载 Qwen2.5-72B-Instruct（~30-60 min via ModelScope，可在 3B 推理时并行）\n5. 跑 72B 推理（~2.5 hr，4 卡 TP）\n6. G-study 分析：对比 3B/8B/72B 方差分解结果\n\n### 风险\n\n1. **磁盘空间紧张** — 72B 模型下载后仅剩 ~55GB，如果中间文件过大可能爆盘。缓解：推理完成后可删除 72B 缓存。\n2. **72B 下载耗时** — AutoDL 代理下 HF 大文件极慢。缓解：用 ModelScope CDN（`modelscope download`）。\n3. **72B 独占 4 卡** — 推理期间无法跑其他实验。安排在非高峰时段。\n4. **vLLM TP 兼容性** — 72B + TP=4 + enforce_eager=True 可能影响性能。可能需关闭 enforce_eager 或调整 `max_model_len`。\n5. **max_model_len** — 72B 的 KV cache 很大；200 条 MMLU prompt（~500 tokens each）需确认不超 GPU 显存。如超限可降低 `gpu_memory_utilization` 或减小 `max_model_len` 到 2048。\n\n### 分析计划\n\nScale validation 成功标准：\n- 3B 和 72B 的方差分量相对比例（prompt > seed > ordering）与 8 个 7B-9B 模型的结论一致\n- 允许绝对值有差异（72B 可能方差更小），但排序应稳定\n- 如果排序不同，需报告为 limitation 并讨论 scale-dependent variance structure\n"
}
